# E357

**Status:** in flight (2026-09-30, wildshard-9: the lead, building GAME-NORMALIZATION v2 from F0; decisions 101–108)
**Ask:** You are the game normalization agent, look at the plan. Audit and review the plan. Compare it against the high level aims and goals I presented. Compare it against the current code in the repo. We need to redo the whole plan. Grill me, ask me any questions or decisions after you finish your audit and review and research

## Jake's context (2026-09-30, verbatim in substance)

- The four shards are generic shards bolted on top of a base game. There is a **base, flexible game engine with
  interfaces**, and anyone (Jake included) can implement a new shard on top of it: a fifth, sixth, seventh.
- A shard is **a directory that implements an interface**, like a plugin. "Almost dynamically loaded", except it isn't,
  for performance, baking and optimization reasons.
- Code shared among shards that doesn't belong in the base engine goes into **a shared library** among shards.
- Any shard `if` in `main.ts` is bad. The base game is generic: it boots and goes directly into whatever makes sense.
- Move as much code as possible out of shard-specific places into the shared baseline (the custom Wildshard engine /
  game logic), and push everything shard-specific into that shard's directory.
- **No other agents work in the repo during the normalization: this session has a lock on the whole repository.**

## Decisions (grill, 2026-09-30)

| # | Question | Jake's answer |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Layout | **Everything under `src/engine/`**, plus `src/kit/` (shared shard library) and `src/shards/<slug>/` |
| 2 | Loading | **Lazy per shard**: defs (data) static, each shard's code one lazy import, prefetched + cached offline |
| 3 | Features | **Rule of two**, and asked why bosses / elites weren't engine → the boundary is **mechanism vs content** |
| 4 | Refactor bar | **Small differences may merge** (before/after board) **and fix found bugs inline** |
| 5 | Boundary | **Mechanism vs content**, and **melee + ranged combat is engine** ("such a strong staple") |
| 6 | Boards | **One board per wave** (weapons, creatures, audio, look); identical steps + bug fixes ship on a green harness |
| 7 | Extras | **All four**: 5th-shard proof (template shard + docs), input actions (ENGINE-FIT E4), delete dead + dev copies, permanent parity gate |
| 8 | Order | **Nine Dragon → Pine Hollow → Nalati → Driftwood** |
| 9 | Scope | **Expanded**: fold a brand-new ENGINE-FIT research in; research AAA + mobile best practice; "we're touching everything engine, do it properly" |

## Handoff (2026-09-30 13:30, CI GPU research)

- **Done:** `348299f2` docs/design/engine-fit-v2/ci-gpu-options.md (150 lines, not pushed). Answer to "Can we get a
  GitHub Actions Mac machine with GPU?": yes, free. Standard `macos-14/15/26` arm64 runners (M1 VM, 3 vCPU, 7 GB) have a
  paravirtualized Metal GPU; full Chromium renders WebGL2/WebGPU on "ANGLE Metal Renderer: Apple Paravirtual device"
  (niivue #199, reallm #42, dunezone #1570, all Sept 2026). Free and unlimited on public repos. Recommended: the blocking
  gpu-gate runs there (phone tier, counts not ms, 4-shard matrix, goldens captured on that runner); Jake's M5 Max runs a
  nightly non-blocking `gpu-perf` via a launchd watcher (never a self-hosted runner); the iPhone stays the memory truth.
  Linux T4 GPU runners need an org on Team ($0.052/min); `-xlarge` M2 Pro $0.102/min; EC2 Mac ~$900+/month.
- **Next:** a free `workflow_dispatch` probe on `macos-15`: boot Driftwood phone tier with `channel: 'chromium'`, log the
  renderer string, boot time, peak RSS and fingerprint. Wildshard's boot time on 3 M1 vCPU is unmeasured.
- **Learned:** Playwright's default headless shell falls back to SwiftShader even on a physical Mac; only
  `channel: 'chromium'` gets Metal, so the gate must assert the renderer. MPS/compute is broken in the VM (raster fine).
  macOS disk is case-insensitive: keep a Linux asset-URL case check. KTX2 format (ASTC/ETC2/BC) and capability limits make
  programs / GPU bytes / pixels renderer-specific: baselines per lane, pin the runner image and Chromium. Cirrus CI shut
  down 2026-06-01. This supersedes the audit's "gate runs on Jake's Mac" (tooling-pipeline-audit §6).
- **Done when:** the plan rewrite picks the gate's lane (recommended: GitHub `macos-15`).

## Handoff (2026-09-30 13:45, budget design research)

- **Done:** `efb4ffbc` docs/design/engine-fit-v2/budget-design.md (200 lines, not pushed). It covers where today's numbers
  came from (§0), studio practice (§1), the target (§2: 30 on the hot phone is what the numbers assume; 60 shown as a
  goal column), the derivation (§3: unit costs → B_frame = T/1.3 → CPU 9.6 / GPU 16.0 ms hot → M5 ruler 1.6 ms), an
  8-sweep one-tap calibration scene (§4), what gates where (§5), and provisional numbers (§6): draws ≤ ~140, tris ≤ 2.0 M,
  programs ≤ 110, GPU MB as a ratchet, per-system CPU and GPU-lane splits, memory 1.8 = L − 0.35 GB, and bytes derived
  from Jake's 4G time caps. §7 is the rollout: a shard over a P number today is ratcheted at its current worst.
- **Learned:** Nine Dragon's 180 / 2.3 M came bottom-up (lane caps ≈ the ex18 measurements, + headroom), with no device
  term. E283 proved counts miss per-pixel cost: 16 fps at 121 draws / 1.1 M. There are three unrelated phone draw budgets
  in the tree (Perf.ts 110 / 1.6 M, PLAY-PERF 150 / 2.0 M, Nine 180 / 2.3 M). Scorecard's bytes rules contradict its own
  time caps (Driftwood's 31.6 MB is 28 s at Fast 4G alone, against a 30 s cap). The draw budget swings 140 ↔ ~500–600 on
  whether WebKit pipelines CPU and GPU (sweep 6): measure that first. Driftwood phone: 264 MB of GL textures, only ~32 MB
  of them material textures. The session's WebSearch budget (200) ran out mid-research. Unverified, so left out: whether
  iOS keeps WebGL texture bytes in the GPU process or in WebContent.

## Handoff (2026-09-30 night, spec 09)

- **Done:** `4ee6a3c7` docs/plans/game-normalization/09-combat-ai.md (913 lines, not pushed): §1 13 weapons + 2 tools
  (id, shard, file:line, family, rung, every tuning number + asset, overrides, upgrades via `equipment.replace`, loadouts,
  `WeaponUi` replacing the 12 weapon-id branch sites, parity tests); §2 attribute sets, 14 effect rows (E1–E14), 10 damage
  rules (R0–R8), AmmoRows, the starter set (proposed values), the full tag vocabulary; §3 `DamageRequest`, every
  player→creature and creature/world→player path, the 5 hurt blocks' replacement, bugs B1–B6 with tests; §4 EventMap /
  AskMap + every hook field mapped; §5 runtime, 24 species (kit/shard, states, tick), 40 strikes S1–S40 with timings,
  4 bosses + 9 elites as rows, director, spawn/loot tables, tick classes; §6 13-step migration; §7 boards; §8 Q1–Q12.
- **Learned:** today every creature brain thinks at 10 Hz at any distance (`AnimalManager.ts:686–699`), so 01 §12's 30 Hz
  near is a behaviour change (kept 10 Hz through S2.3, rates at S2.6). GoldenBow and Naizagai are upgrade objects applied
  to live instances, not subclasses. The Titan cap/guard fix (B3) is invisible today (Nalati sets no cap, has no tusk).
  `canReach` gaps also cover Nalati elites and the Golden King (they call `onCharge` directly). The big crab's
  `chargeDamage 14` is dead data. Falls don't reset the regen delay. The javelin "camp upgrade → 5" has no code.
  Hit-stop is per shard today but each weapon lives on one shard, so per-weapon rows are identical.
- **Open questions:** Q1 StrikeSpec shape params; Q2 DamageRuleDef row type; Q3 weapon-scoped attributes; Q4 10 vs 30 Hz;
  Q5 horse kit vs Nalati; Q6 fall regen; Q7 hoverboard as a kit Tool; Q8 big crab 10 vs 14; Q9 WeightedTable `'each'`;
  Q10 finishes as cosmetic effects; Q11 DamageRequest superset; Q12 javelins 3.
- **Done when:** the lead answers Q1–Q12 (01 edits for Q1–Q3, Q9, Q11) and the council reviews 09.

| 92 | Council protocol | **All eight**: a frozen ledger (decisions + lead resolutions can be reopened only with new evidence that they're wrong against the code or contradict another settled item); one bar (an executor would fail, do the wrong thing or have to guess; evidence + a fix required; taste = nit); a cross-round findings register with stable IDs; round 1 reviews everything, later rounds review the diff + the battery; a fixed battery that only grows; plan-lint for the mechanical checks; minimal fixes citing a finding ID; accepted must+should counts must fall each round or the loop stops and escalates |
| 93 | Round cap | **4 rounds**; if two clean rounds haven't happened by then, what's still open comes to Jake as decisions (one recommended answer each), then the plan goes `ready` |

  body/sound/listener/timer/RAF/disposer kinds. Failures aggregate after all cleanup. Late ownership is freed immediately.
  AssetService.register(key,resource,{retain?}) seeds its generic resource registry; retained() snapshots every held key
  with refs and the engine-retained flag; last release frees only nonretained resources. No resource loader invented.
  GameClock.paused/timeScale exclude paused time and slow simulation; capture uses 1/fps for both now and real deltas
  (pause/scale still affect now), tick returns the unscaled delta. App has events and only typed optional clock/assets.
